Everything just changed after that.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the time right after your bowling partner died—say, in those first two months—did you notice yourself feeling sad and down during that period, even if you can't remember exactly when it started?\n\nPatient: I suppose I must have been sad, yes. He was my friend. But... if you're asking if it was this exact same darkness back in those first two months, I just don't know. Like I said, it all just runs together in my head now. I really can't break it down month by month for you.\n\nDoctor: In those first two weeks right after your bowling partner died, would you say you were functioning okay—able to get out of bed, eat, take care of yourself—or were you already having significant difficulty with daily activities back then?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I just... I just know things have been very bad for a long time since he passed. I can't give you a day-by-day breakdown of how I was functioning right at the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Looking back now, do you think it took at least two or three months after your bowling partner died before you really started feeling this darkness and these thoughts about not wanting to wake up, or had it set in by then?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I really can't say for sure. I don't know if it was two months or three months, or if it was sooner. Like I keep saying, the whole last year is just a blur of... of feeling this way. I guess that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times in your life when you felt unusually energetic, needed very little sleep, or felt like your thoughts were racing—times that were quite different from how you're feeling now?\n\nPatient: No... no, I can't say I've ever felt like that. I've never been the type to have endless energy or go without sleep. My mind doesn't really race, it just... well, no. I haven't ever experienced anything like what you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods like this before—times when you felt depressed, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like the ones you're having now—at other points in your life?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't. I mean, over eighty years you have your normal ups and downs, but... no. Nothing like this. I've never felt this low or had these kinds of thoughts before. This is really the first time it's ever been like this.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling this way? Can you tell me approximately how long you've been experiencing these feelings of depression and these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it started... gosh, about a year and a month ago. That's when my bowling partner passed away. Ever since then, things have just been... like this.\n\nDoctor: When your bowling partner passed away a year ago, did you start feeling this way right after that happened, or did it take some time—weeks or months—before these feelings of depression and these thoughts about not wanting to wake up really set in?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not entirely sure, to be completely honest with you. It's hard to remember the exact timeline. I just know that after he passed, things started going downhill. I can't really say if it hit me right away or... or if it took a few weeks for it to get this heavy. It all just kind of blends together now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back now, would you say these feelings of depression and thoughts about not wanting to wake up started within the first few weeks after your bowling partner passed away, or did they come on more gradually over months after that?\n\nPatient: I really don't know. Like I said, it's... it's all just a blur. I can't pinpoint if it was right after or if it took a while. I just know he died, and then... well, then I ended up like this.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to those first few weeks after your bowling partner died, do you remember feeling noticeably sad or down during that time, or did you feel relatively okay back then and only start feeling this way much later?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I really can't tell you. I know you're trying to figure out exactly when it started, but my memory of that time just isn't that clear. I don't remember if I was crying the first week or if it took a month before I couldn't get out of bed. I just know he's gone, and I've been in this dark place for a long time. That's really all I can say about it.\n\nDoctor: Before your bowling partner passed away a year ago, would you say you were feeling okay—sleeping well, enjoying activities, not having dark thoughts—or were you already struggling with your mood back then?\n\nPatient: I was fine before that. I was sleeping okay, going about my life. But this... this has been going on since he died. That was about a year and a month ago. Everything just changed after that.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the time right after your bowling partner died—say, in those first two months—did you notice yourself feeling sad and down during that period, even if you can't remember exactly when it started?\n\nPatient: I suppose I must have been sad, yes. He was my friend. But... if you're asking if it was this exact same darkness back in those first two months, I just don't know. Like I said, it all just runs together in my head now. I really can't break it down month by month for you.\n\nDoctor: In those first two weeks right after your bowling partner died, would you say you were functioning okay—able to get out of bed, eat, take care of yourself—or were you already having significant difficulty with daily activities back then?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I just... I don't know. Like I told you, I really can't remember those first couple of weeks clearly. I don't know if I was getting out of bed or eating right away, or if that came later. I don't know exactly when the really dark thoughts started, just that they've been there for a long while now.\n\nDoctor: Let me ask this differently: if you had to guess, would you say that within the first two months after your bowling partner died, you were already having these dark thoughts and feeling this level of depression, or do you think it took longer than two months before you really felt this way?\n\nPatient: I know you're trying to pin down a specific timeline here, but... I just know he died, and then... well, then I ended up like this.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to those first few weeks after your bowling partner died, do you remember feeling noticeably sad or down during that time, or did you feel relatively okay back then and only start feeling this way much later?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I really can't tell you. I just know he died, and then... well, then I ended up like this.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to those first few weeks after your bowling partner died, do you remember feeling noticeably sad or down during that time, or did you feel relatively okay back then and only start feeling this way much later?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I really can't tell you. I know you're trying to figure out exactly when it started, but my memory of that time just isn't that clear. I don't remember if I was crying the first week or if it took a month before I couldn't get out of bed. I just know he's gone, and I've been in this dark place for a long time. That's really all I can say about it.\n\nDoctor: Before your bowling partner passed away a year ago, would you say you were feeling okay—sleeping well, enjoying activities, not having dark thoughts—or were you already struggling with your mood back then?\n\nPatient: I was fine before that. I was sleeping okay, going about my life. But this... this has been going on since he died. That was about a year and a month ago. Everything just changed after that.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the time right after your bowling partner died—say, in those first two months—did you notice yourself feeling sad and down during that period, even if you can't remember exactly when it started?\n\nPatient: I suppose I must have been sad, yes. He was my friend. But... if you're asking if it was this exact same darkness back in those first two months, I just don't know. Like I said, it all just runs together in my head now. I really can't break it down month by month for you.\n\nDoctor: In those first two weeks right after your bowling partner died, would you say you were functioning okay—able to get out of bed, eat, take care of yourself—or were you already having significant difficulty with daily activities back then?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I just... I don't know. Like I told you, I really can't remember those first couple of weeks clearly. I don't know if I was getting out of bed or eating right away, or if that came later. I just know things have been very bad for a long time since he passed. I can't give you a day-by-day breakdown of how I was functioning right at the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Looking back now, do you think it took at least two or three months after your bowling partner died before you really started feeling this darkness and these thoughts about not wanting to wake up, or had it set in by then?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I really can't say for sure. I don't know if it was two months or three months, or if it was sooner. Like I keep saying, the whole last year is just a blur of... of feeling this way. I don't know exactly when the really dark thoughts started, just that they've been there for a long while now.\n\nDoctor: Let me ask this differently: if you had to guess, would you say that within the first two months after your bowling partner died, you were already having these dark thoughts and feeling this level of depression, or do you think it took longer than two months before you really felt this way?\n\nPatient: I know you're trying to pin down a specific timeline here, but... I really couldn't even give you a guess. Whether it was inside those first two months or after... I honestly just don't know. \n\nMy memory of that whole period is just... it's gone. I can't separate the months out to tell you when it got this bad.\n\nDoctor: Let me try asking this one more way: When you think about three months after your bowling partner died, do you remember that time as a period when you were already feeling this same darkness and having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up, or does it feel like you were still doing somewhat better back then?\n\nPatient: Doc, I know you're trying every which way to get a timeline out of me, but... I just don't have it. Looking back at that three-month mark, I really can't tell you if I was doing somewhat better or if I was already wanting to just stay asleep. \n\nIt's all just one big, dark cloud in my head. I'm sorry, I just don't remember when exactly it got this heavy. It all just blends together.\n\nDoctor: I understand the timeline is blurry for you. My wife has had to step in and take over everything—the bills, keeping the calendars, making all the decisions that I always used to handle. I just can't manage it. I guess I've been withdrawn for a while, and my family and my pastor... well, they got scared enough by what I was saying and doing that they finally pushed me to come get urgent help.\n\nDoctor: When your bowling partner first passed away, did you start having these dark thoughts and this level of depression within about the first two months, or did it take considerably longer—like several months or more—before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Like I said before, I really don't know. I can't tell you if it was within the first two months or if it took considerably longer. I just don't have that timeline in my head. It's all just a blur since he passed. I haven't ever experienced anything like what you're describing.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced periods like this before—times when you felt depressed, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like the ones you're having now—at other points in your life?\n\nPatient: No... no, I haven't. I mean, over eighty years you have your normal ups and downs, but... no. Nothing like this. I've never felt this low or had these kinds of thoughts before. This is really the first time it's ever been like this.\n\nDoctor: When did you first start feeling this way? Can you tell me approximately how long you've been experiencing these feelings of depression and these thoughts about not wanting to wake up?\n\nPatient: Well, I'd say it started... gosh, about a year and a month ago. That's when my bowling partner passed away. Ever since then, things have just been... like this.\n\nDoctor: When your bowling partner passed away a year ago, did you start feeling this way right after that happened, or did it take some time—weeks or months—before these feelings of depression and these thoughts about not wanting to wake up really set in?\n\nPatient: I... I'm not entirely sure, to be completely honest with you. It's hard to remember the exact timeline. I just know that after he passed, things started going downhill. I can't really say if it hit me right away or... or if it took a few weeks for it to get this heavy. It all just kind of blends together now.\n\nDoctor: Looking back now, would you say these feelings of depression and thoughts about not wanting to wake up started within the first few weeks after your bowling partner passed away, or did they come on more gradually over months after that?\n\nPatient: I really don't know. Like I said, it's... it's all just a blur. I can't pinpoint if it was right after or if it took a while. I just know he died, and then... well, then I ended up like this.\n\nDoctor: When you think back to those first few weeks after your bowling partner died, do you remember feeling noticeably sad or down during that time, or did you feel relatively okay back then and only start feeling this way much later?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I really can't tell you. I know you're trying to figure out exactly when it started, but my memory of that time just isn't that clear. I don't remember if I was crying the first week or if it took a month before I couldn't get out of bed. I just know he's gone, and I've been in this dark place for a long time. That's really all I can say about it.\n\nDoctor: Before your bowling partner passed away a year ago, would you say you were feeling okay—sleeping well, enjoying activities, not having dark thoughts—or were you already struggling with your mood back then?\n\nPatient: I was fine before that. I was sleeping okay, going about my life. But this... this has been going on since he died. That was about a year and a month ago. Everything just changed after that.\n\nDoctor: When you think about the time right after your bowling partner died—say, in those first two months—did you notice yourself feeling sad and down during that period, even if you can't remember exactly when it started?\n\nPatient: I suppose I must have been sad, yes. He was my friend. But... if you're asking if it was this exact same darkness back in those first two months, I just don't know. Like I said, it all just runs together in my head now. I really can't break it down month by month for you.\n\nDoctor: In those first two weeks right after your bowling partner died, would you say you were functioning okay—able to get out of bed, eat, take care of yourself—or were you already having significant difficulty with daily activities back then?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I just... I don't know. Like I told you, I really can't remember those first couple of weeks clearly. I don't know if I was getting out of bed or eating right away, or if that came later. I just know things have been very bad for a long time since he passed. I can't give you a day-by-day breakdown of how I was functioning right at the beginning.\n\nDoctor: Looking back now, do you think it took at least two or three months after your bowling partner died before you really started feeling this darkness and these thoughts about not wanting to wake up, or had it set in by then?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I really can't say for sure. I don't know if it was two months or three months, or if it was sooner. Like I keep saying, the whole last year is just a blur of... of feeling this way. I don't know exactly when the really dark thoughts started, just that they've been there for a long while now.\n\nDoctor: Let me ask this differently: if you had to guess, would you say that within the first two months after your bowling partner died, you were already having these dark thoughts and feeling this level of depression, or do you think it took longer than two months before you really felt this way?\n\nPatient: I know you're trying to pin down a specific timeline here, but... I really couldn't even give you a guess. Whether it was inside those first two months or after... I honestly just don't know. \n\nMy memory of that whole period is just... it's gone. I wish I could give you a better answer, but I just know it's been heavy for a very long time now. I can't separate the months out to tell you when it got this bad.\n\nDoctor: Let me try asking this one more way: When you think about three months after your bowling partner died, do you remember that time as a period when you were already feeling this same darkness and having these thoughts about not wanting to wake up, or does it feel like you were still doing somewhat better back then?\n\nPatient: Doc, I know you're trying every which way to get a timeline out of me, but... I just don't have it. Looking back at that three-month mark, I really can't tell you if I was doing somewhat better or if I was already wanting to just stay asleep. \n\nIt's all just one big, dark cloud in my head. I'm sorry, I just don't remember when exactly it got this heavy. It all just blends together.\n\nDoctor: I understand the timeline is blurry for you. But let me ask this simply: In the months right after your bowling partner died—say around month two or three—were you able to go about your day and do things, or were you already mostly staying in bed and withdrawn like you are now?\n\nPatient: Well, I still can't give you the exact month it happened, but I can tell you I am definitely not going about my day anymore. \n\nI've just... I've completely stopped being dependable, both at the senior center and at home. I don't do any of the things I used to. My wife has had to step in and take over everything—the bills, keeping the calendars, making all the decisions that I always used to handle. I just can't manage it. I guess I've been withdrawn for a while, and my family and my pastor... well, they got scared enough by what I was saying and doing that they finally pushed me to come get urgent help.\n\nDoctor: When your bowling partner first passed away, did you start having these dark thoughts and this level of depression within about the first two months, or did it take considerably longer—like several months or more—before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Like I said before, I really don't know. I can't tell you if it was within the first two months or if it took considerably longer. I just don't have that timeline in my head. It's all just a blur since he passed. I'm sorry, I just don't know the answer to that.\n\nDoctor: I know the timeline is fuzzy for you, but let me ask you this: When you think about the first month or two after your bowling partner died, do you remember yourself as someone who was grieving but still basically functioning—still able to handle things—or were you already in this same dark, withdrawn state that you're in now?\n\nPatient: Doc, I... I really don't mean to be difficult here. But I honestly can't tell you. \n\nI don't remember if I was still handling things in that first month or two, or if I had already given up and retreated to bed. My memory of that whole period is just a blank. I just don't have a clear picture of myself back then to give you. I'm sorry, I just don't know.\n\nDoctor: In that first month after your bowling partner died, do you remember going to the senior center or doing activities with your wife, or had you already stopped doing those things by then?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I... I really don't know how else to explain this to you. I just don't remember. I don't know if I went to the senior center that first month, or if I did things with my wife, or if I had already stopped. It's like my mind just wiped all that time away. I'm sorry, but I just don't have the answer.\n\nDoctor: Looking back now, in that first month after your bowling partner died, did you notice a sudden change—like you woke up one day and everything felt dark and hopeless—or did the sadness and dark feelings come on more slowly and gradually over time?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I know you're trying to piece this together, but... it's exactly like I've been saying. I really have no idea. \n\nI don't know if it hit me all at once one morning, or if it crept up on me slow. That whole month is just gone from my head. I just don't remember how it started.\n\nDoctor: I understand the timeline is hard to remember. Let me ask you this: In that very first week or two right after your bowling partner died, before anything else happened, do you remember feeling okay—like you were sad about losing him, but you were still sleeping, still eating, still getting up and doing things—or do you not remember feeling okay at any point after he died?\n\nPatient: Doctor, I'm really trying here, but... I just can't give you a different answer. I don't remember that first week or two either. I don't know if I was eating or sleeping or doing anything normal right after he passed. It's all just a blur. I'm sorry, I really am, but I just don't have those memories.\n\nDoctor: I know you've said the timeline is blurry, but let me ask you this way: In the very first days right after your bowling partner died, before a week had even passed, do you remember feeling any different than you normally did, or does that first week feel like it was just part of the blur?\n\nPatient: It really is just part of the blur. I know you're trying to figure out the exact timing of all this, but I just can't separate those first few days from the rest of it. It's all just a heavy fog. I don't remember feeling one way or another right at the beginning.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The prolonged course and severity favor F32.9 over F43.21 (Adjustment Disorder with depressed mood), as adjustment disorders typically resolve within 6 months of stressor removal or within 6 months of onset.",
